This proposition seems relevant to Acro's situation.

TURN 4: 3-1-1_Turnabout_Memories (Turn 1) [RUN: 20250806_001504]
============================================================

PROMPT TYPE: main_contradiction_detection
--------------------

LLM INPUT (PROMPT SENT):
------------------------------
## INPUT PROVIDED:
You are provided with:
- A list of characters, evidences and testimonies
- A set of spatial propositions for analysis

## SPATIAL PROPOSITIONS:
Prop 1: If a person is described as being in the same location as an object at the time of an event, and later evidence places the object in a different location, the person must have moved or the object was moved after the event.
Prop 2: If an object is found covered by another object, and the covered object is described as having fallen from above, the covering object must have been above the covered object at the time of the fall.
Prop 3: If a person is said to be facing another person or object, and there is an obstruction between them, the person cannot see the other person or object unless the obstruction is transparent or the obstruction is described as being moved.
Prop 4: If a person is described as entering a location and exiting from another, and there is a wall or barrier between these points, the person must have passed through an opening or the wall/barrier must have been removed or bypassed.
Prop 5: If a person is described as being in a room where a crime occurred, and another piece of evidence suggests the crime happened outside the room, the person's presence must be reconciled with the location of the crime or the description of the crime scene must be incorrect.

---

Story:
 The witness Dahlia is describing how she met Phoenix Wright, the defendant, on August 27, 8 years ago, in the courthouse basement. We know from before that on the same day at the same location, a muder happened.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

Dahlia's testimonies describe her meeting Phoenix Wright in the courthouse basement on August 27, 8 years ago. However, Evidence 2 and Evidence 3 indicate that the crime involving Doug Swallow took place outside the courthouse, specifically behind an Ivy U. building. This creates a spatial contradiction because Dahlia's account places her inside the courthouse basement, while the crime scene is described as being outside.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can apply Proposition 5 to this situation.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 5 states: "If a person is described as being in a room where a crime occurred, and another piece of evidence suggests the crime happened outside the room, the person's presence must be reconciled with the location of the crime or the description of the crime scene must be incorrect."

In this case, Dahlia describes herself as being in the courthouse basement on August 27, but the crime involving Doug Swallow is described as happening outside the courthouse. This directly contradicts her claim about her whereabouts on that date.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Not applicable in this case as Proposition 5 applies.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 2, "testimony": 0}

- Testimony 4 mentions that there is dirt inside the monster costume, suggesting that the body was hidden inside the costume, which implies that the body was likely in contact with the dirt.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

- Proposition 3 states: If an object is found clutched in a person's hand and the person was facing up when found, then the object was placed in the person's hand after they were positioned face up.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- Proposition 3 applies here because it suggests that if the body was found face-up and there was no dirt on the front of the body, then anything that might have been placed on or near the body (like the costume) would have had to be done after the body was positioned face-up. This contradicts the idea that the body was hidden inside the costume, as the dirt inside the costume would logically transfer onto the front of the body if it was placed there after the body was already face-up.

- In this case, Proposition 3 does apply and provides the necessary spatial reasoning to identify the contradiction.

Proposition 4 states: "If a person knocks an object over and it lands on another object, then the knocked-over object must have been above the other object before falling."

However, the Crime Photo (Evidence 9) shows that the guitar case does not have water stains, indicating that the vase did not land on it. This contradicts the spatial relationship implied by Proposition 4.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Spatial reasoning indicates that if the vase had fallen directly onto the guitar case, the case would have been stained with water. Since the guitar case is not stained, it suggests that the vase did not fall onto the guitar case, contradicting Andrews' claim.

Prop 4: If a key card opens a door and a record shows it was used at a certain time, the person who owns the key card must be in the vicinity of the door at that time.
Prop 5: If a person is described as being at a location that is decently far away from another location, and evidence suggests movement from one location to another occurred within a short timeframe, the person's testimony about their presence at the distant location is questionable.

This contradicts the idea that the president was already dead and the crime scene was already set up when Judge Gavèlle left the roof.

TURN 13: 10-4-2_A_Turnabout_Forsaken (Turn 1) [RUN: 20250806_001504]
============================================================

PROMPT TYPE: main_contradiction_detection
--------------------

LLM INPUT (PROMPT SENT):
------------------------------
## INPUT PROVIDED:
You are provided with:
- A list of characters, evidences and testimonies
- A set of spatial propositions for analysis

## SPATIAL PROPOSITIONS:
Prop 1: If a person is inside a location and that location is covered by another location, the person inside cannot be seen from outside the covering location.
Prop 2: If an object is found inside a box and the box is located in a room, the object must have been placed in the box while the box was in that room.
Prop 3: If blood is detected on an elevator and it drips to the floor below, the elevator must have been moving downward at the time the blood was deposited.
Prop 4: If a person is described as leaving a room through a specific exit and that exit is blocked by a wall, the person could not have left through that exit.
Prop 5: If a weapon is found in a storeroom and the storeroom is disconnected from the viewing platform, the weapon could not have been used on the viewing platform without being moved.

According to Evidence 7, the Bigg Building pamphlet, the concession stand is located to the right-most edge of the viewing platform, while the cherry tree is to the left-most edge. This implies that if someone was walking towards Kay from the other side of the concession stand, they would be coming from the right side of the platform relative to the cherry tree.

However, Kay also mentions feeling like she saw something red under the cherry tree (Testimony 3), which suggests that she was facing the cherry tree when she saw the red object. This creates a potential spatial contradiction because if Kay was facing the cherry tree, she would not be able to see someone coming from the other side of the concession stand, which is to the right of the cherry tree.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us understand or resolve this contradiction. Proposition 1 is relevant as it deals with the spatial relationship between the concession stand and the cherry tree.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If a person is pushed off a building from behind a concession stand, then the person pushing must be to the right of the cherry tree if the stand is to the right of the tree."

Given that the concession stand is to the right of the cherry tree, if Kay was pushed off the building from behind the concession stand, the person pushing her must be to the right of the cherry tree. However, Kay's testimony (Testimony 3) suggests she was facing the cherry tree when she saw something red, which contradicts the idea that she could see someone coming from the other side of the concession stand (Testimony 4).

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does apply and helps us identify the contradiction. Kay's testimonies suggest conflicting positions relative to the cherry tree and the concession stand, indicating a spatial inconsistency.

Prop 1: If a person is inside a location and that location is covered by another object, then the person is also covered by that object
Prop 2: If a weapon is used to shoot a person from a specific direction, then the person will fall in the opposite direction of the shot
Prop 3: If a person is seen entering a location and then exiting from a different location, then the original entry location must contain a path leading to the exit location
Prop 4: If a person is described as being in front of an object and another person is behind the same object, then the two people are facing each other across the object
Prop 5: If a person is found with an object in their possession and that object was previously inside a different location, then the person must have accessed that location at some point

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Phoenix states that he shoved Doug, causing Doug to fall on his umbrella, resulting in a loud noise. This suggests that the umbrella broke due to Doug falling on it. Additionally, Evidence 9 mentions that the old power cable broke due to some sort of impact at 2:55 PM, which is before the time of death.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can see that Proposition 3 might apply here because it deals with a loud noise occurring after someone is shoved and an object being found broken at the crime scene.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 3 states that if a person hears a loud noise immediately after shoving another person, and an object owned by the shoved person is found broken at the crime scene, then the loud noise may have been caused by the breaking of that object. In this case, Phoenix heard a loud noise after shoving Doug, and Doug's umbrella was found broken at the crime scene. This would suggest that the loud noise was caused by the breaking of the umbrella.

However, the timing of events contradicts this. According to Evidence 9, the power cable broke at 2:55 PM, which is before the time of death (3 PM). This implies that the loud noise Phoenix heard could not have been the breaking of the umbrella but rather the breaking of the power cable.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 3 does apply, but the contradiction arises from the timing of events. Phoenix's testimony (Testimony 6) that he returned to find Doug dead at 3:05 PM conflicts with the autopsy report (Evidence 1) stating the time of death as 3 PM. Furthermore, the breaking of the power cable at 2:55 PM (Evidence 9) suggests that the loud noise Phoenix heard was likely due to the power cable breaking, not the umbrella.

Proposition 2 states: "If an object is wet and muddy, and a location is dry and clean, then the object cannot be found at that location without being altered." This proposition applies to our situation because the scarf (wet and muddy) and the victim's coat (dry and clean) are both associated with the same incident.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 2 does apply, so we don't need to use additional spatial reasoning beyond what the proposition provides.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 4, "testimony": 3}

{
  "evidence": 4,
  "testimony": 3

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Dahlia's testimony states that she was shoved off the bridge from behind by Terry Fawles. According to Evidence 6 (Witness's Photo), the victim (Valerie) was standing close to the broken end of the bridge, facing left, and a man in jail uniform and chains (presumably Terry Fawles) was facing her from the left. This implies that Terry Fawles would have been positioned to the left of Valerie, not behind her.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if a person is shoved off a bridge from behind, they fall downward towards the river below. Proposition 2 states that if a person is facing left while standing close to the broken end of a bridge, they are near the edge and any object to their right is also near the broken end.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 does not directly apply since it describes the outcome of being shoved from behind, not the positioning. However, Proposition 2 does apply because it indicates that if Valerie was facing left and standing close to the broken end, then anything to her right would also be near the broken end. Since Terry Fawles was facing Valerie from the left, he could not have been behind her to shove her off the bridge.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 2 helps us understand the spatial arrangement. If Valerie was facing left and Terry Fawles was facing her from the left, he would be in front of her, not behind her. Therefore, the claim that Terry Fawles shoved her off the bridge from behind contradicts the spatial positioning shown in the photo.

This proposition applies to the situation where the defendant's prints on the bottle are upside-down. It suggests that the defendant was somehow inverted when handling the bottle, which is unusual and could imply that the defendant was not in a normal upright position while committing the act.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 2 does apply, and it reveals that the defendant's interaction with the bottle was not typical, indicating a possible inconsistency in the story about the murder.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, L'Belle states that he fled down the hallway to the right where there is a phone at the end. This implies that L'Belle moved from the crime scene to the end of the hallway with the phone. According to Evidence 5, the Crime Scene Diagram, the hallway to the north ends with a dead end and a phone, suggesting that L'Belle fled towards the north.

However, Evidence 12, Jinxie's Statement, mentions that she saw the demon (which L'Belle claims to be) flee towards the foyer. According to the Crime Scene Diagram (Evidence 5), the foyer is located to the south of the hallway intersection.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can apply Proposition 1 to analyze the movement of L'Belle and Proposition 2 to understand visibility between rooms.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if a person flees down a hallway to the right and reaches a location with a phone at the end, then that person is no longer in the original location they started fleeing from. This proposition supports L'Belle's claim that he fled to the location with the phone.

Proposition 2 states that if an object is inside a room and a wall separates that room from another, then the object cannot be seen from the other room unless there is a door or window allowing visibility. This proposition helps us understand that Jinxie, who was in the foyer, could not have seen L'Belle fleeing towards the north end of the hallway unless there was a door or window connecting the two locations.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Propositions 1 and 2 do apply. Proposition 1 confirms L'Belle's movement away from the crime scene, while Proposition 2 indicates that Jinxie's view of the hallway from the foyer would be obstructed by walls unless there were openings such as doors or windows. Since the diagrams do not indicate any such openings, Jinxie's statement that she saw the demon (L'Belle) fleeing towards the foyer contradicts L'Belle's testimony about fleeing towards the north end of the hallway.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 5, "testimony": 12}

{

  "testimony": 12

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions apply to the contradiction identified in Step 1.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If a person is covered by objects inside a room, then they are not visible from outside the room through the window."

This proposition applies because according to the Study Photo (Evidence 3), Dr. Buff is covered by books, which would mean he should not be visible from outside the room through the window. However, Atishon claims he saw the murder happen from outside the study window (Testimony 1).

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction between these two statements.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

This proposition does not directly apply because it talks about the weapon being directly above the body, whereas Ema mentions the weapon hitting from behind.

However, Proposition 1 states: "If a person is hit from behind with a weapon and falls forward, the weapon must be behind the person when the attack occurs." This proposition applies to the scenario where the victim was hit from behind and fell into the lantern. According to this proposition, the attacker must have been behind the victim during the attack.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 does apply. The contradiction arises because Ema's statement that the victim was hit from behind and fell into the lantern implies the attacker was behind the victim. Yet, Ema also states that the defendant was seen standing in front of the body holding the weapon, which contradicts the idea that the attacker was behind the victim.

TURN 52: 9-3-1_The_Kidnapped_Turnabout (Turn 1) [RUN: 20250806_001504]

--------------------

------------------------------

You are provided with:
- A list of characters, evidences and testimonies
- A set of spatial propositions for analysis

## SPATIAL PROPOSITIONS:
Prop 1: If a person is shot from above and the body is found with minimal blood at the site, then the body must have been moved from its original position.
Prop 2: If a weapon is found inside a location and a person is shot outside that location, then the weapon must have been taken out of the location before the shooting occurred.
Prop 3: If a person is facing a wall and another person is behind that wall, then the first person cannot see the second person.
Prop 4: If an object is found broken and used to block a door, then the object must have been intact and in a different location prior to being used.
Prop 5: If a person is wearing a fully masked costume and shows signs of being shot at close range, then the shooter must have been within close proximity to the victim.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we learn that Officer Meekins (Mike Meekins) shot Oliver Deacon (Mr. Deacon) from on top of the stage. This implies that the shooting took place from above. From the evidence, specifically Evidence 6 (Murder Notes), we know that the entry point of the bullet was the lower right abdomen and the exit point was the right shoulder. Additionally, the notes mention that despite the fatal bullet going clean through the body, there is very little blood at the site where the body was found, indicating that the body was moved.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can apply Proposition 1 to this scenario, which states that if a person is shot from above and the body is found with minimal blood at the site, then the body must have been moved from its original position.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 applies here. According to the testimonies, Officer Meekins shot Deacon from above, which aligns with the bullet trajectory described in the Murder Notes. However, the spatial proposition suggests that if the shooting happened from above, the body would have been found with minimal blood at the site because it was moved. This means that the shooting did not occur where the body was found.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises because the testimonies suggest that the shooting occurred on top of the stage, implying the body was shot in situ. However, the evidence indicates that the body was found elsewhere with minimal blood, suggesting it had been moved after the shooting. This contradicts the idea that the body was shot and left where it was found according to the testimonies.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 6, "testimony": 2}

--------------------
{
  "evidence": 6,
  "testimony": 2

From the testimonies, Nash and Ringo claim that they were inside the pawnbrokery when they heard a gunshot and that they were not involved in the shooting. They also mention that they heard someone yelling, "Gimme that gun!" before the gunshot. 

Evidence 12 states that the gun belonging to the victim, Mr Windibank, was found in Gina's possession when she was found unconscious. Evidence 10, the Crime Scene Photograph, shows the victim with a single bullet wound in his back. 

Given these testimonies and evidence, there seems to be a spatial contradiction regarding the position of Gina and the gun. If Gina was holding the gun when she was found unconscious, and the Skulkin brothers were inside the pawnbrokery when the gunshot occurred, it raises questions about the possibility of Gina being the shooter or the victim herself.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can apply Proposition 4 to this scenario, which states that if a person is holding an object and another person is shot, the holder of the object cannot simultaneously be the shooter if the object obstructs the line of fire.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 4 applies here. According to the evidence, Gina was holding the gun when she was found unconscious. If she was holding the gun, it would obstruct her line of fire, making it impossible for her to shoot Mr Windibank from behind him. However, the autopsy report (Evidence 9) indicates that Mr Windibank died from a bullet wound to the back, suggesting he was shot from behind.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 4 does apply, and it reveals that Gina could not have been the shooter if she was holding the gun in such a way that it obstructed her line of fire towards Mr Windibank. This contradicts the spatial arrangement implied by the autopsy report and the crime scene photograph, which suggest a shooter positioned behind Mr Windibank.
